and similarly for
g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
. Since
\nabla\phi(G_{n})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and
\nabla\phi
is a continuous bijection (SA2), we have:

\nabla\phi(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}),

so
\tilde{G}_{n+1},G_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

MP-OR Type A:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(G_{n})\big),\quad G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\big).

Since
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
,
\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\in L^{2}
, so:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}),\quad G_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X).

MP-OR Type B:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(G_{n})\big),\quad Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\big),\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}.

Since
Y_{n}\in L^{2}
(as above), and
\lambda_{n}\in(0,2)
,
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}
. By induction,
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Mapping to Algorithm
1
.

Algorithm
1
requires an update:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}U_{n}u_{n}^{*},

with an outer-approximation condition:

\langle z,E[U_{n}u_{n}^{*}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Stochastic Mirror-Prox:
Set
\lambda_{n}=1
,
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
. The update:

G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\big),

matches Algorithm
1
. Define the auxiliary term:

U_{n}\eta_{n}=\eta_{n}\langle\tilde{G}_{n+1},g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\rangle-\eta_{n}\langle z,g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})=g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-T(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
. Since
T
is monotone,
\langle T(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-T(z),\tilde{G}_{n+1}-z\rangle\geq 0
, and:

\langle z,E[\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle=\eta_{n}\langle z,T(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\rangle\leq\eta_{n}\langle\tilde{G}_{n+1},T(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Set
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
, satisfying the outer-approximation condition.

MP-OR Type A
: Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
, with relaxation
\lambda_{n}
. The update:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),

matches Algorithm
1
. The outer-approximation condition holds with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

MP-OR Type B
: Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
. The KM step:

G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n},\quad Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\big(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\big),

is equivalent to the dual update via convex combinations, satisfying Algorithm
1
.

Super-relaxation and factorization.

For over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\}\cup\Phi_{n})
. Lemma
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\cdot E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}],

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B or MP) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A). This ensures the descent term is appropriately scaled for convergence analysis.
∎

C.3.12
Proof of Theorem
4.6
(Convergence of (OR) Mirror-Prox)

Proof.

Assume
Z\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n},\tilde{G}_{n+1}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5). By Proposition
B.5
, Stochastic Mirror-Prox (MP) and MP-OR are special cases of Algorithm
1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(MP or Type B) or
\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Convergence under deterministic
z
.

Assume
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (MP or Type B), or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (Type A), and
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
g_{n}(G_{n})
: For MP, Proposition
B.5
gives:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\langle g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

For MP-OR, the inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(MP or Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s., Theorem
3.3
(part 1a) and Lemma
3.1
imply:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

With
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and the summability conditions, we have
\sum_{n}E[\Theta_{n}]<\infty
. Since
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. and
g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
converges similarly (as
\tilde{G}_{n+1}\to G
), the dominated convergence theorem implies
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
: By Theorem
3.3
(part 1c), since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s.,
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with strong monotonicity
: If
T
is strongly monotone with modulus
\mu>0
, i.e.,
\langle T(x)-T(y),x-y\rangle\geq\mu\|x-y\|_{X}^{2}
, then
\langle g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-g_{n}(G_{n}),\tilde{G}_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle\geq\mu\|\tilde{G}_{n+1}-G_{n}\|_{X}^{2}
. Theorem
3.3
(part i.d) implies strong convergence a.s. to
G
.

Convergence under random
z
.

Assume
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
g_{n}(G_{n})
: By Theorem
3.3
(part 2a), since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, Hölder’s inequality and the dominated convergence theorem imply
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence
: By Theorem
3.3
(part ii.c), since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
G\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
and
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with strong monotonicity
: If
T
is strongly monotone, Theorem
3.3
(part ii.d) ensures
G_{n}\to G
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

Convergence rates.

If
T
is strongly monotone with modulus
\mu>0
, and
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
, Proposition
B.1
gives a polynomial rate
ED_{\phi}(G_{n},G)=O(1/n)
. For MP-OR, if the contraction condition of Theorem
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+cY_{n}(\cdot,z),\quad\chi\in(0,1),

then a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G_{n},G)=O(\chi^{n})
applies.
∎

C.3.13
Proof of Proposition
B.6
(Mirror Descent and Over-relaxed Mirror Descent under Relative Smoothness)

Proof.

Assume
Z\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space,
\phi
is Legendre (proper, lower semicontinuous, essentially smooth, and strictly convex),
Z=\arg\min f
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
, and
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
. For over-relaxed variants, assume
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
. We need to show that both standard Mirror Descent (
algorithm

4
) and its over-relaxed variants (
algorithm

5
) are special cases of
algorithm

1
, with convergence rates under relative strong convexity.

Well-definedness.

Prove by induction that
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Standard Mirror Descent (
algorithm

4
):
Initialize
G_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi)
. Assume
G_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n},

where
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
. Since
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s., by Hölder’s inequality:

E\|\eta_{n}g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}=E\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\leq\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty,

so
\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. Since
\nabla\phi(G_{n})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
(by continuity of
\nabla\phi
), we have:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}).

As
\phi
is Legendre,
\nabla\phi:\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi\to X^{*}
is a continuous bijection, so:

G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X).

By induction,
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Over-relaxed Mirror Descent (
algorithm

5
, Type B):
The update is:

Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}\right),\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}.

As above,
Y_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
since
\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}
. Given
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
,
1-\lambda_{n}\in(-1,1)
, so
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
as a convex combination of
G_{n}
and
Y_{n}
, both in
L^{2}
. By induction,
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. (Type A follows similarly by setting
\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
.)

Mapping to Algorithm
1
.

The update in
algorithm

1
is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}U_{n}u_{n}^{*},

with the outer-approximation condition:

\langle z,E[U_{n}u_{n}^{*}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Standard MD:
Set
\lambda_{n}=1
,
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
, where
u_{n}^{*}=g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and
U_{n}=\eta_{n}
. The update becomes:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n},

matching
algorithm

4
. The relative smoothness condition gives:

f(G_{n+1})\leq f(G_{n})+\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle+LD_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n}).

Since
g_{n}
is a subgradient,
\langle g_{n},G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle\leq\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle
. The MD update minimizes:

G_{n+1}=\arg\min_{y}\left[D_{\phi}(y,G_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},y-G_{n}\rangle\right],

yielding
\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})-\nabla\phi(G_{n})+\eta_{n}g_{n}=0
. Using the Bregman divergence property:

D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})=D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})+\langle\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\nabla\phi(G_{n+1}),z-G_{n+1}\rangle,

substitute
\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}
:

\langle\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\nabla\phi(G_{n+1}),z-G_{n+1}\rangle=\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},z-G_{n+1}\rangle.

Thus:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E[D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},z-G_{n+1}\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Since
g_{n}
is an unbiased subgradient (
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
), and using relative smoothness:

E[\langle g_{n},G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq E[f(G_{n+1})-f(G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq LE[D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Define
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
, where
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
. Then:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

satisfying the outer-approximation condition with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Over-relaxed MD (Type B):
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
,
u_{n}^{*}=g_{n}
, and adjust with
\lambda_{n}
. The update is:

Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}),\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}.

This can be rewritten in the dual space to match
algorithm

1
by expressing the convex combination. Using the Bregman projection:

D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\lambda_{n})D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+\lambda_{n}D_{\phi}(z,Y_{n})-\lambda_{n}(1-\lambda_{n})D_{\phi}(Y_{n},G_{n}).

For
Y_{n}
, apply the standard MD analysis:

D_{\phi}(z,Y_{n})\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Thus:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)-\lambda_{n}(1-\lambda_{n})E[D_{\phi}(Y_{n},G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Set
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
, and note that
-\lambda_{n}(1-\lambda_{n})E[D_{\phi}(Y_{n},G_{n})]\leq 0
since
\lambda_{n}\in(0,2)
. The outer-approximation condition holds, and
\lambda_{n}
enhances the descent term via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
(by
3.1
).

Over-relaxed MD (Type A):
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n},

directly matching
algorithm

1
with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. The analysis follows similarly.

Convergence Rates under Relative Strong Convexity.

If
f
is relatively strongly convex with modulus
\sigma>0
, i.e.,
D_{f}(z,x)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(z,x)
, then for
z\in Z
(where
\nabla f(z)=0
):

\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z).

For standard MD:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z)+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\eta_{n}\sigma)ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

With
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives a polynomial rate
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
.

For over-relaxed MD, using
3.1
:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z).

If
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
, and assuming a contraction condition from
3.2
(part iv.c):

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
when
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
.

Thus, both standard and over-relaxed Mirror Descent are special cases of
algorithm

1
, with polynomial rates (
O(1/n)
) under relative strong convexity and geometric rates (
O(\chi^{n})
) under the contraction condition of
3.2
.
∎

C.3.14
Proof of Theorem
4.7
(Convergence of Mirror Descent under Relative Smoothness)

Proof.

Assume
Z\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space,
\phi
is Legendre (proper, lower semicontinuous, essentially smooth, and strictly convex),
Z=\arg\min f
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
, and
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
. For over-relaxed variants (
algorithm

5
), assume
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
, and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
. By
B.6
, both standard Mirror Descent (
algorithm

4
) and its over-relaxed variants (
algorithm

5
) are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
(standard MD or Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard MD) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed MD), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for
algorithm

4
or
algorithm

5
Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for
algorithm

5
Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard MD,
B.6
gives the Bregman-Fejér inequality:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
, we have
E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, and by convexity of
f
,
\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq 0
. Since
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
(part v) implies:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

By Hölder’s inequality:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\leq\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}.

Since
(G_{n})
is Bregman-bounded a.s. (
3.1
, part v.a),
\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}
is bounded a.s., and
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. implies
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. By the dominated convergence theorem for Bochner integrals
[
Dinculeanu 2000
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

For over-relaxed MD,
B.6
gives:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A). Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\sum_{n}\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. (as
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}
), and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
,
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Thus,
3.3
(part i.a) implies:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (noting
E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\approx E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}
under bounded noise),
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
:

For both standard and over-relaxed MD, since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s. (
A.1
),
3.1
(part v.d) and
3.3
(part i.c) imply
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with strong convexity or demiregularity:

If
f
is relatively strongly convex with modulus
\sigma>0
, i.e.,
D_{f}(z,x)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(z,x)
, then for
z\in Z
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z).

For standard MD:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z)+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\eta_{n}\sigma)ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
, and
3.1
(part v.e) ensures
G_{n}\to G
strongly a.s.

For over-relaxed MD, the descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
, and
3.3
(part i.d) similarly ensures strong convergence a.s. with rate
O(1/n)
. If
\nabla f
is demiregular on
Z
(i.e.,
x_{n}\rightharpoonup z
and
\nabla f(x_{n})\to 0
imply
x_{n}\to z
), then
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. (part (a)) and
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G
(part (b)) imply
G_{n}\to G
strongly a.s. by
A.4
.

Convergence under random
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard MD,
3.1
(part vi.a) gives
L^{2}
-boundedness of
(G_{n})
, and:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, we have:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]<\infty.

By Hölder’s inequality:

E|\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle|\leq E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\leq\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}\sqrt{E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}}.

Since
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and a.s. by Lemma
A.3
.

For over-relaxed MD,
3.3
(part ii.a) gives:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]<\infty,

implying
\sum_{n}E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty
a.s., and thus
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence:

Since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
3.1
(part vi.b) and
3.3
(part ii.c) ensure
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded, and if
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s., then
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with strong convexity or demiregularity:

If
f
is relatively strongly convex, the rate analysis from part (i.c) extends to random
z
, giving
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
and strong convergence in
L^{1}
and a.s. (
3.1
, part vi.e). If
\nabla f
is demiregular,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s. (part (a)) and
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G
(part (b)) imply
G_{n}\to G
in
L^{1}
and a.s. by
A.4
.

Geometric rates under contraction.

If
f
is relatively strongly convex and the contraction condition of
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j}.

Since
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
,
3.2
(part iv.c) ensures
G_{n}\to G
strongly in
L^{2}
and a.s., with:

E\|G_{n}-G\|_{X}^{2}\leq 4\chi^{n}Ed_{Z,\phi}^{2}(G_{0})+4c\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\chi^{n-j-1}E\psi_{j}+2c\sum_{j\geq n}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G,G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
for both standard and over-relaxed MD, with over-relaxation enhancing the descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.
∎

C.4
Proofs of Section
5
(Applications)

C.4.1
Proof of Proposition
B.7
(Bregman Perspective on Regularized and Over-Relaxed ERM)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min(f+h)\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space,
\phi
is Legendre (proper, lower semicontinuous, essentially smooth, and strictly convex),
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. We need to show that all updates (standard, Type A, and Type B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
, satisfy the outer-approximation condition of
A.3
, inherit the Bregman–Fejér descent of
3.1
, and, for over-relaxed variants, admit the expectation factorization of
3.1
.

Well-definedness.

Prove by induction that
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Standard Prox-SGD:
Initialize
G_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi)
. Assume
G_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. The update is:

G_{n+1}\in\arg\min_{y\in X}\left\{D_{\phi}(y,G_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},y-G_{n}\rangle+\eta_{n}h(y)\right\}.

The first-order optimality condition gives:

0\in\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})-\nabla\phi(G_{n})+\eta_{n}g_{n}+\eta_{n}\partial h(G_{n+1}),

so there exists
\xi_{n+1}\in\partial h(G_{n+1})
such that:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}).

Since
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, by Hölder’s inequality:

E\|\eta_{n}g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}=E\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\leq\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty,

so
\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. Assume
h
has a Lipschitz-continuous subgradient (e.g., for
\ell_{1}
or
\ell_{\infty}
norms), ensuring
\xi_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
since
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}
(to be confirmed). Thus,
\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. Since
\nabla\phi(G_{n})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
, we have:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}).

As
\phi
is Legendre,
\nabla\phi:\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi\to X^{*}
is a continuous bijection, so:

G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}))\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X).

Type A Over-Relaxed:
Assume
G_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}),

where
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
. By Hölder’s inequality:

E\|\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}\leq 4\|\lambda_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}E\|\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty,

so
\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. Thus:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}),

and
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
The update is:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}\in\arg\min_{y\in X}\left\{D_{\phi}(y,G_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},y-G_{n}\rangle+\eta_{n}h(y)\right\},\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}\tilde{G}_{n+1}.

From the standard Prox-SGD analysis,
\tilde{G}_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. Since
\lambda_{n}\in(0,2)
,
1-\lambda_{n}\in(-1,1)
, and both
G_{n},\tilde{G}_{n+1}\in L^{2}
, we have
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. By induction,
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
for all variants.

Mapping to Algorithm
1
.

The update in
algorithm

1
is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}U_{n}u_{n}^{*},

with the outer-approximation condition:

\langle z,E[U_{n}u_{n}^{*}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Standard Prox-SGD:
Set
\lambda_{n}=1
,
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})
, where
u_{n}^{*}=g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. The update becomes:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}),

matching
algorithm

1
. Define:

U_{n}\eta_{n}=\eta_{n}\langle G_{n},g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\rangle-\eta_{n}\langle z,g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
. For
z\in Z
, where
0\in\nabla f(z)+\partial h(z)
, there exists
\zeta\in\partial h(z)
such that
\nabla f(z)+\zeta=0
. Since
f+h
is convex:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle\geq(f+h)(G_{n})-(f+h)(z)\geq 0.

Taking expectations:

E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\approx\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta,G_{n}-z\rangle+E[\langle\xi_{n+1}-\zeta,G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Assuming bounded noise, set
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
, satisfying:

\langle z,E[\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[\eta_{n}\langle G_{n},\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z).

Type A Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})
, with
\lambda_{n}
as the relaxation parameter. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}),

matching
algorithm

1
. The optimality condition is:

0\in\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})-\nabla\phi(G_{n})+\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}+\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}\partial h(G_{n+1}).

The outer-approximation condition holds with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
, as above.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\tilde{\xi}_{n+1})
, where
\tilde{\xi}_{n+1}\in\partial h(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
. The proximal substep gives:

\nabla\phi(\tilde{G}_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\tilde{\xi}_{n+1}).

The update
G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}\tilde{G}_{n+1}
approximates:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})\approx\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\tilde{\xi}_{n+1}),

matching
algorithm

1
via convex combinations. The outer-approximation condition holds with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Bregman–Fejér Property.

For all variants, the Bregman-Fejér condition (
A.1
) requires:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\Psi_{n}+o_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},

with
\Psi_{n},o_{n}\geq 0
. For standard Prox-SGD, using the Bregman divergence identity:

D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})=D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})+\langle\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\nabla\phi(G_{n+1}),z-G_{n+1}\rangle,

substitute
\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})
:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E[D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},z-G_{n+1}\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Since
G_{n+1}
minimizes
D_{\phi}(y,G_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},y-G_{n}\rangle+\eta_{n}h(y)
:

E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq-E[D_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]/\eta_{n}.

Thus:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Set
\Psi_{n}=\eta_{n}\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle
,
o_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta,G_{n}-z\rangle\geq 0
for
z\in Z
,
\Psi_{n}\geq 0
a.s.

For over-relaxed variants, the inequality becomes:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A) or
\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}+\tilde{\xi}_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. The descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
. Assuming
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (standard/Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (Type A),
3.1
and
3.3
ensure convergence.

Factorization for Over-Relaxed Variants.

Since
\lambda_{n}
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
,
3.1
applies:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}],

enhancing descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
. The summability conditions ensure convergence via
3.3
.

∎

C.4.2
Proof of Theorem
5.1
(Convergence of Sparse Bregman-SGD)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min(f+h)\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space,
\phi
is Legendre (proper, lower semicontinuous, essentially smooth, and strictly convex),
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. By
B.7
, standard Prox-SGD and over-relaxed Prox-SGD (Type A or B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})
(standard or Type B, with
\xi_{n+1}\in\partial h(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
for Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}(g_{n}+\xi_{n+1})
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for standard or Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}
:

For standard Prox-SGD (
B.7
), the Bregman-Fejér inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
and
\xi_{n+1}\approx\zeta_{n}\in\partial h(G_{n})
(assuming bounded subgradient variation), we have:

E[\langle g_{n}+\xi_{n+1},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\approx\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle.

For
z\in Z
, where
0\in\nabla f(z)+\partial h(z)
, there exists
\zeta\in\partial h(z)
such that
\nabla f(z)+\zeta=0
. By convexity of
f+h
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\geq(f+h)(G_{n})-(f+h)(z)\geq 0.

Since
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
(part v) implies:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

By Hölder’s inequality:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\leq\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}.

Since
(G_{n})
is Bregman-bounded a.s. (
3.1
, part v.a),
\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}
is bounded a.s., and
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. implies
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\to 0
a.s. By the dominated convergence theorem
[
Dinculeanu 2000
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

For over-relaxed Prox-SGD (
B.7
), the inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\sum_{n}\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. (as
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}
), and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
,
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Thus,
3.3
(part i.a) implies:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (noting
E\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}\approx E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
),
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
:

For both standard and over-relaxed Prox-SGD, since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s. (
A.1
),
3.1
(part v.d) and
3.3
(part i.c) imply
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with strong convexity:

If
f+h
is relatively strongly convex w.r.t.
\phi
with modulus
\sigma>0
, i.e.,
D_{f+h}(z,x)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(z,x)
, then for
z\in Z
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f+h}(G_{n},z)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z).

For standard Prox-SGD:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z)+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\eta_{n}\sigma)ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
, and
3.1
(part v.e) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly a.s.

For over-relaxed Prox-SGD, the descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
, and
3.3
(part i.d) ensures strong convergence a.s. with rate
O(1/n)
, potentially with improved constants due to
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
.

Convergence under random
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}
:

For standard Prox-SGD,
3.1
(part vi.a) gives
L^{2}
-boundedness of
(G_{n})
, and:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle]+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle]<\infty.

By Hölder’s inequality:

E|\langle\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle|\leq E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\leq\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}}\sqrt{E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}}.

Since
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
,
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\to 0
in
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and a.s. by Lemma
A.3
.

For over-relaxed Prox-SGD,
3.3
(part ii.a) gives:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]<\infty,

implying
\sum_{n}E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}+\xi_{n+1}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty
a.s., and thus
\nabla f(G_{n})+\zeta_{n}\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence:

Since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
3.1
(part vi.b) and
3.3
(part ii.c) ensure
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded, and if
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s., then
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with strong convexity:

If
f+h
is relatively strongly convex, the rate analysis from part (i.c) extends to random
z
, giving
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
and strong convergence in
L^{1}
and a.s. (
3.1
, part vi.e;
3.3
, part ii.d).

Geometric rates under contraction.

If
f+h
is relatively strongly convex and the contraction condition of
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j}.

Since
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
,
3.2
(part iv.c) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly in
L^{2}
and a.s., with:

E\|G_{n}-G^{\star}\|_{X}^{2}\leq 4\chi^{n}Ed_{Z,\phi}^{2}(G_{0})+4c\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\chi^{n-j-1}E\psi_{j}+2c\sum_{j\geq n}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G^{\star},G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
for both standard and over-relaxed Prox-SGD, with over-relaxation enhancing the descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.

∎

C.4.3
Proof of Proposition
B.8
(Cross-Entropy as Relatively Smooth for SMD)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min f\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space (here, the probability simplex
X=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid x_{i}\geq 0,\sum_{i}x_{i}=1\}
),
\phi(x)=\sum_{i}x_{i}\log x_{i}
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. We need to show that
f
is relatively smooth w.r.t.
\phi
, that all updates (standard, Type A, Type B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
, satisfy the outer-approximation condition of
A.3
, inherit the Bregman–Fejér descent of
3.1
with rates via
3.2
, and, for over-relaxed variants, admit the expectation factorization of
3.1
.

Relative Smoothness.

Verify that
\phi(x)=\sum_{i}x_{i}\log x_{i}
is Legendre on
X
. It is proper (defined on
X
), lower semicontinuous (continuous on
\operatorname{int}X
), essentially smooth (gradient
\nabla\phi(x)=(1+\log x_{i})_{i}
diverges as
x_{i}\to 0^{+}
), and strictly convex (Hessian
\nabla^{2}\phi(x)=\text{diag}(1/x_{i})
is positive definite), satisfying SA2. The Bregman divergence is:

D_{\phi}(y,x)=\sum_{i}y_{i}\log\frac{y_{i}}{x_{i}}-\sum_{i}(y_{i}-x_{i})=\sum_{i}y_{i}\log\frac{y_{i}}{x_{i}},

the KL divergence. The cross-entropy loss is:

f(x)=-\sum_{i}q_{i}\log p_{i}(x),\quad p_{i}(x)=\frac{\exp(x_{i})}{\sum_{j}\exp(x_{j})},

where
q
is the true label distribution. The gradient is
\nabla f(x)=p(x)-q
. To show
f
is
L
-smooth relative to
\phi
:

f(y)\leq f(x)+\langle\nabla f(x),y-x\rangle+LD_{\phi}(y,x).

Consider the second-order Taylor expansion of
f
:

f(y)=f(x)+\langle\nabla f(x),y-x\rangle+\frac{1}{2}(y-x)^{T}\nabla^{2}f(x)(y-x)+o(\|y-x\|^{2}).

The Hessian is
\nabla^{2}f(x)=\text{diag}(p(x))-p(x)p(x)^{T}
, positive semi-definite. Since
\nabla^{2}\phi(x)=\text{diag}(1/x_{i})
, we need
\nabla^{2}f(x)\preceq L\nabla^{2}\phi(x)
. The eigenvalues of
\nabla^{2}f(x)
are bounded (maximum eigenvalue
\leq 1
for softmax), and
\nabla^{2}\phi(x)
has eigenvalues
1/x_{i}
. Assuming standard network conditions (e.g., bounded inputs, Lipschitz softmax), there exists
L>0
such that:

\langle\nabla f(y)-\nabla f(x),y-x\rangle\leq LD_{\phi}(y,x),

derived from the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla f
relative to
D_{\phi}
on the simplex.

Well-definedness.

Prove by induction that
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Standard SMD:
Initialize
G_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi)
. Assume
G_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}.

Since
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}
, by Hölder’s inequality:

E\|\eta_{n}g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}=E\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\leq\|\eta_{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty,

so
\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}
. Since
\nabla\phi(G_{n})\in L^{2}
, we have:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*}).

As
\phi
is Legendre,
\nabla\phi
is a continuous bijection, so
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}
.

Type A Over-Relaxed:
The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}.

Since
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
,
\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}
, and
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}
.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
The update is:

Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}\right),\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}.

Since
Y_{n}\in L^{2}
(as in standard SMD), and
\lambda_{n}\in(0,2)
,
G_{n+1}\in L^{2}
. By induction,
(G_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
for all variants.

Mapping to Algorithm
1
.

The update in
algorithm

1
is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}U_{n}u_{n}^{*},

with the outer-approximation condition:

\langle z,E[U_{n}u_{n}^{*}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Standard SMD:
Set
\lambda_{n}=1
,
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
. The update matches
algorithm

1
. Since
f
is relatively smooth:

f(G_{n+1})\leq f(G_{n})+\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n+1}-G_{n}\rangle+LD_{\phi}(G_{n+1},G_{n}).

Define
U_{n}\eta_{n}=\eta_{n}\langle G_{n}-z,g_{n}\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
, where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, so:

E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq 0.

Thus:

\langle z,E[\eta_{n}g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[\eta_{n}\langle G_{n},\nabla f(G_{n})\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

satisfying the outer-approximation condition with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Type A Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
, with
\lambda_{n}
as the relaxation parameter. The update is:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n},

matching
algorithm

1
. The outer-approximation condition holds with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
. The update
G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}
approximates:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})\approx\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n},

with the outer-approximation condition satisfied using
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Bregman–Fejér Property.

For all variants, the Bregman-Fejér condition (
A.1
) requires:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\Psi_{n}+o_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},

with
\Psi_{n},o_{n}\geq 0
. For standard SMD:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

with
\Psi_{n}=\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle
,
o_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq 0
,
\Psi_{n}\geq 0
a.s. For over-relaxed variants:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
, and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Assuming
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
and
3.3
ensure convergence, with rates via
3.2
under strong convexity.

Factorization for Over-Relaxed Variants.

Since
\lambda_{n}
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
,
3.1
applies:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}],

enhancing descent by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
for faster convergence in deep network training.
∎

C.4.4
Proof of Theorem
5.2
(Bregman-Accelerated Training for Cross-Entropy Loss)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min f\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is the probability simplex
\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid x_{i}\geq 0,\sum_{i}x_{i}=1\}
,
\phi(x)=\sum_{i}x_{i}\log x_{i}
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. By
B.8
, standard SMD and over-relaxed SMD (Type A or Type B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
(standard or Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for standard or Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard SMD (
B.8
), the Bregman-Fejér inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
, we have
E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, and by convexity of
f
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq 0.

Since
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
(part v) implies:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

By Hölder’s inequality:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\leq\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}.

Since
(G_{n})
is Bregman-bounded a.s. (
3.1
, part v.a),
\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}
is bounded a.s., and
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. implies
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. By the dominated convergence theorem
[
Dinculeanu 2000
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

For over-relaxed SMD (
B.8
), the inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\sum_{n}\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. (as
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}
), and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
,
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Thus,
3.3
(part i.a) implies:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (noting
E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\approx E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}
),
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
:

For both standard and over-relaxed SMD, since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s. (
A.1
),
3.1
(part v.d) and
3.3
(part i.c) imply
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with strong convexity:

If
f
is relatively strongly convex w.r.t.
\phi
with modulus
\sigma>0
, i.e.,
D_{f}(z,x)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(z,x)
, then for
z\in Z
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z).

For standard SMD:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z)+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\eta_{n}\sigma)ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
, and
3.1
(part v.e) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly a.s.

For over-relaxed SMD, the descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
, and
3.3
(part i.d) ensures strong convergence a.s. with rate
O(1/n)
, potentially with improved constants due to
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
.

Convergence under random
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard SMD,
3.1
(part vi.a) gives
L^{2}
-boundedness of
(G_{n})
, and:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]<\infty.

By Hölder’s inequality:

E|\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle|\leq E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\leq\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}\sqrt{E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}}.

Since
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and a.s. by Lemma
A.3
.

For over-relaxed SMD,
3.3
(part ii.a) gives:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]<\infty,

implying
\sum_{n}E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty
a.s., and thus
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence:

Since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
3.1
(part vi.b) and
3.3
(part ii.c) ensure
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded, and if
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s., then
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with strong convexity:

If
f
is relatively strongly convex, the rate analysis from part (i.c) extends to random
z
, giving
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
and strong convergence in
L^{1}
and a.s. (
3.1
, part vi.e;
3.3
, part ii.d).

Geometric rates under contraction.

If
f
is relatively strongly convex and the contraction condition of
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j}.

Since
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
,
3.2
(part iv.c) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly in
L^{2}
and a.s., with:

E\|G_{n}-G^{\star}\|_{X}^{2}\leq 4\chi^{n}Ed_{Z,\phi}^{2}(G_{0})+4c\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\chi^{n-j-1}E\psi_{j}+2c\sum_{j\geq n}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G^{\star},G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
for both standard and over-relaxed SMD, with over-relaxation enhancing the descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.

∎

C.4.5
Proof of Proposition
B.9
(KL-Constrained Policy Update for TRPO/PPO)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min f\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
\pi_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is the probability simplex
\{\pi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid\pi_{i}\geq 0,\sum_{i}\pi_{i}=1\}
,
\phi(\pi)=\sum_{i}\pi_{i}\log\pi_{i}
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(\pi_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{\pi_{0},\ldots,\pi_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. We need to show that all updates (standard, Type A, Type B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
, satisfy the outer-approximation condition of
A.3
, inherit the Bregman–Fejér descent of
3.1
, and, for over-relaxed variants, admit the expectation factorization of
3.1
.

Well-definedness.

Prove by induction that
(\pi_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Standard Update:
Initialize
\pi_{0}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi)
. Assume
\pi_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
. The update is:

\pi_{n+1}\in\arg\min_{\pi}\left\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},\pi-\pi_{n}\rangle\mid D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})\leq\delta\right\}.

This is equivalent to:

\pi_{n+1}=\arg\min_{\pi}\left\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},\pi-\pi_{n}\rangle+I_{\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})\leq\delta\}}(\pi)\right\},

where
I_{\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})\leq\delta\}}
is the indicator function. The first-order optimality condition gives:

0\in\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})-\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})+\eta_{n}g_{n}+\mu_{n}(\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})-\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})),

where
\mu_{n}\geq 0
is the Lagrange multiplier. Rearranging:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})=\frac{1}{1+\mu_{n}}\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}.

Since
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}
, and
\mu_{n}\geq 0
, the update term is in
L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
. As
\phi
is Legendre,
\nabla\phi
is a continuous bijection, so
\pi_{n+1}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
.

Type A Over-Relaxed:
The update is:

\pi_{n+1}\in\arg\min_{\pi}\left\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})+\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},\pi-\pi_{n}\rangle\mid D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})\leq\delta\right\}.

The optimality condition is:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})=\frac{1}{1+\mu_{n}}\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\frac{\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}.

Since
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
,
\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}\in L^{2}
, and
\pi_{n+1}\in L^{2}
.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
The update is:

\tilde{\pi}_{n+1}\in\arg\min_{\pi}\left\{D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})+\eta_{n}\langle g_{n},\pi-\pi_{n}\rangle\mid D_{\phi}(\pi,\pi_{n})\leq\delta\right\},\quad\pi_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})\pi_{n}+\lambda_{n}\tilde{\pi}_{n+1}.

Since
\tilde{\pi}_{n+1}\in L^{2}
(as in standard update), and
\lambda_{n}\in(0,2)
,
\pi_{n+1}\in L^{2}
. By induction,
(\pi_{n})\subset L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X)
for all variants.

Mapping to Algorithm
1
.

The update in
algorithm

1
is:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\lambda_{n}U_{n}u_{n}^{*},

with the outer-approximation condition:

\langle z,E[U_{n}u_{n}^{*}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\rangle\leq E[U_{n}\eta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Standard Update:
Set
\lambda_{n}=1
,
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}
. The update becomes:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n},

matching
algorithm

1
. Define:

U_{n}\eta_{n}=\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}\langle\pi_{n}-z,g_{n}\rangle+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|\pi_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(\pi_{n})
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, so:

E[\langle g_{n},\pi_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(\pi_{n}),\pi_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(\pi_{n},z)\geq 0.

Thus:

\langle z,E\left[\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]\rangle\leq E\left[\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}\langle\pi_{n},\nabla f(\pi_{n})\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}\right]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

satisfying the outer-approximation condition with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Type A Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}
, with
\lambda_{n}
as the relaxation parameter. The update is:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\lambda_{n}\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n},

with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Type B Over-Relaxed:
Set
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n}
. The update
\pi_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})\pi_{n}+\lambda_{n}\tilde{\pi}_{n+1}
approximates:

\nabla\phi(\pi_{n+1})\approx\nabla\phi(\pi_{n})-\lambda_{n}\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}g_{n},

with
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
.

Bregman–Fejér Property.

For all variants, the Bregman-Fejér condition (
A.1
) requires:

E[D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n})-\Psi_{n}+o_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},

with
\Psi_{n},o_{n}\geq 0
. For standard update:

E[D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n})-\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}E[\langle g_{n},\pi_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z),

with
\Psi_{n}=\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}\langle g_{n},\pi_{n}-z\rangle
,
o_{n}=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. For over-relaxed variants:

E[D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,\pi_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z),

where
\Theta_{n}=\left(\frac{\eta_{n}}{1+\mu_{n}}\right)^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
, and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Assuming
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
and
3.3
ensure convergence.

Factorization for Over-Relaxed Variants.

Since
\lambda_{n}
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
,
3.1
applies:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}],

enhancing descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.

∎

C.4.6
Proof of Theorem
5.3
(Mirror-Prox for Actor–Critic)

Proof.

Assume
Z\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is a separable reflexive Banach space,
\phi
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}(\cdot)\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic oracle for
\nabla f
, with
E[g_{n}(y)\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(y)
a.s.,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. By
B.5
, standard Mirror-Prox and over-relaxed Mirror-Prox (Type A or Type B) are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(standard or Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})=g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for standard or Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
g_{n}(G_{n})
:

For standard Mirror-Prox (
B.5
), the Bregman-Fejér inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
E[g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
, we have
E[\langle g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, and by monotonicity:

\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-\nabla f(z),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq 0.

Since
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
(part v) implies:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

By Hölder’s inequality:

\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle\leq\|\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}.

Since
(G_{n})
is Bregman-bounded a.s. (
3.1
, part v.a),
\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}
is bounded a.s., and
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. implies
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. By the dominated convergence theorem
[
Dinculeanu 2000
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

For over-relaxed Mirror-Prox (
B.5
), the inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\sum_{n}\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. (as
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}
), and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
,
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Thus,
3.3
(part i.a) implies:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (noting
E\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}\approx E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}
under bounded variation),
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
:

For both standard and over-relaxed Mirror-Prox, since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s. (
A.1
),
3.1
(part v.d) and
3.3
(part i.c) imply
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with strong monotonicity:

If
f
is strongly monotone with modulus
\mu>0
, i.e.,
\langle\nabla f(G)-\nabla f(G^{\prime}),G-G^{\prime}\rangle\geq\mu\|G-G^{\prime}\|_{X}^{2}
, then for
z\in Z
:

\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-\nabla f(z),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq\mu\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}.

For standard Mirror-Prox:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\mu\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\mu E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
, and
3.1
(part v.e) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly a.s.

For over-relaxed Mirror-Prox, the descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
, and
3.3
(part i.d) ensures strong convergence a.s. with rate
O(1/n)
, potentially with improved constants due to
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
.

Convergence under random
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

Assume
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
g_{n}(G_{n})
:

For standard Mirror-Prox,
3.1
(part vi.a) gives
L^{2}
-boundedness of
(G_{n})
, and:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle]+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle]<\infty.

By Hölder’s inequality:

E|\langle\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1}),G_{n}-z\rangle|\leq E\|\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\leq\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}}\sqrt{E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}}.

Since
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
,
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and a.s. by Lemma
A.3
.

For over-relaxed Mirror-Prox,
3.3
(part ii.a) gives:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]<\infty,

implying
\sum_{n}E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty
a.s., and thus
g_{n}(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence:

Since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
3.1
(part vi.b) and
3.3
(part ii.c) ensure
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded, and if
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s., then
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with strong monotonicity:

If
f
is strongly monotone, the rate analysis from part (i.c) extends to random
z
, giving
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
and strong convergence in
L^{1}
and a.s. (
3.1
, part vi.e;
3.3
, part ii.d).

Geometric rates under contraction.

If
f
is strongly monotone and the contraction condition of
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j}.

Since
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
,
3.2
(part iv.c) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly in
L^{2}
and a.s., with:

E\|G_{n}-G^{\star}\|_{X}^{2}\leq 4\chi^{n}Ed_{Z,\phi}^{2}(G_{0})+4c\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\chi^{n-j-1}E\psi_{j}+2c\sum_{j\geq n}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G^{\star},G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
for both standard and over-relaxed Mirror-Prox, with over-relaxation enhancing the descent via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.

∎

C.4.7
Proof of Theorem
5.4
(Convergence of SMD, AdaGrad, and RMSProp in LLMs)

Proof.

Assume
Z=\arg\min f\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is the probability simplex
\{p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid p_{i}\geq 0,\sum_{i}p_{i}=1\}
,
\phi(p)=\sum_{i}p_{i}\log p_{i}
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. The updates are:

•

Standard SMD:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}
,

•

Over-Relaxed SMD Type A:

\nabla\phi(G_{n+1})=\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
,

•

Over-Relaxed SMD Type B:

Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}\right)
,
G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}
,

•

AdaGrad:

\eta_{n}=\eta/\sqrt{v_{n}+\epsilon}
,
v_{n}=\sum_{k\leq n}\|g_{k}\|_{*}^{2}
,

•

RMSProp:

\eta_{n}=\eta/\sqrt{v_{n}+\epsilon}
,
v_{n}=\rho v_{n-1}+(1-\rho)\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
,

with over-relaxed AdaGrad/RMSProp using
\lambda_{n}
. By
B.10
, all updates are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}
(standard or Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}=g_{n}-\nabla f(G_{n})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for standard SMD, AdaGrad, RMSProp, or Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard SMD, AdaGrad, or RMSProp (
B.10
), the Bregman-Fejér inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
E[g_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(G_{n})
, we have
E[\langle g_{n},G_{n}-z\rangle\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle
. For
z\in Z
,
\nabla f(z)=0
, and by convexity of
f
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq 0.

Since
\sum_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s.,
3.1
(part v) implies:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

By Hölder’s inequality:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\leq\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}.

Since
(G_{n})
is Bregman-bounded a.s. (
3.1
, part v.a),
\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}
is bounded a.s., and
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s. implies
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. By the dominated convergence theorem
[
Dinculeanu 2000
, Theorem 2.3.5]
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

For over-relaxed variants, the inequality is:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+Y_{n}(\cdot,z)\quad\text{a.s.},

where
\Theta_{n}=\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type B) or
(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
. Since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\sum_{n}\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. (as
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}
), and
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]>0
,
3.1
gives:

E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\Theta_{n}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}].

Thus,
3.3
(part i.a) implies:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]=\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty\quad\text{a.s.}

Since
\inf_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})]\geq\epsilon>0
, and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (noting
E\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}\approx E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}
),
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
strongly a.s. in
X^{*}
.

(b) Weak convergence of
G_{n}
:

For all variants, since
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. and
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s. (
A.1
),
3.1
(part v.d) and
3.3
(part i.c) imply
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in Z
a.s.

(c) Strong convergence with demiregularity or strong convexity:

If
\nabla f
is demiregular on
Z
(i.e.,
x_{n}\rightharpoonup z\in Z
and
\nabla f(x_{n})\to 0
imply
x_{n}\to z
), then
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
a.s. (part (a)) and
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}
(part (b)) imply
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly a.s. by
A.4
. If
f
is relatively strongly convex w.r.t.
\phi
with modulus
\sigma>0
, i.e.,
D_{f}(z,x)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(z,x)
, then for
z\in Z
:

\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle\geq D_{f}(G_{n},z)\geq\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z).

For standard variants:

E[D_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq D_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}\sigma D_{\phi}(G_{n},z)+\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Taking expectations:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq(1-\eta_{n}\sigma)ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\eta_{n}\asymp 1/n
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}=\infty
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
, Proposition
A.2
gives
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
, and
3.1
(part v.e) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly a.s. For over-relaxed variants, the descent term is amplified by
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
, and
3.3
(part i.d) ensures strong convergence a.s. with rate
O(1/n)
, potentially with improved constants.

Convergence under random
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

Assume
E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
, and
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
for all
z\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{X}_{0},P;Z)
.

(a) Strong convergence of
\nabla f(G_{n})
:

For standard variants,
3.1
(part vi.a) gives
L^{2}
-boundedness of
(G_{n})
, and:

ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n+1})\leq ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})-\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]+E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z).

Since
\sum_{n}E\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
:

\sum_{n}\eta_{n}E[\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle]<\infty.

By Hölder’s inequality:

E|\langle\nabla f(G_{n}),G_{n}-z\rangle|\leq E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\leq\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}\sqrt{E\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}^{2}}.

Since
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded and
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}\sqrt{E\|\nabla f(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}}<\infty
,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
and a.s. by Lemma
A.3
.

For over-relaxed variants,
3.3
(part ii.a) gives:

\sum_{n}E[\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})\Theta_{n}]<\infty,

implying
\sum_{n}E[\eta_{n}^{2}\|g_{n}\|_{*}^{2}]<\infty
a.s., and thus
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s.

(b) Weak
L^{2}
convergence:

Since
\sum_{n}EY_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
,
3.1
(part vi.b) and
3.3
(part ii.c) ensure
(G_{n})
is
L^{2}
-bounded, and if
\mathfrak{W}(G_{n})\subset Z
a.s., then
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;Z)
in
L^{2}
and a.s.

(c) Strong
L^{1}
convergence with demiregularity or strong convexity:

If
\nabla f
is demiregular,
\nabla f(G_{n})\to 0
in
L^{1}
and a.s. (part (a)) and
G_{n}\rightharpoonup G^{\star}
(part (b)) imply
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
in
L^{1}
and a.s. by
A.4
. If
f
is relatively strongly convex, the rate analysis from part (i.c) extends to random
z
, giving
ED_{\phi}(z,G_{n})=O(1/n)
and strong convergence in
L^{1}
and a.s. (
3.1
, part vi.e;
3.3
, part ii.d).

Geometric rates under contraction.

If
f
is relatively strongly convex and the contraction condition of
3.2
(part iv.c) holds:

E[d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]\leq\chi d_{Z,\phi}(G_{n})+c\psi_{n}\quad\text{a.s.},\quad\chi\in(0,1),

where
\psi_{n}=Y_{n}(\cdot,0)
, then:

Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{n+1})\leq\chi^{n+1}Ed_{Z,\phi}(G_{0})+c\sum_{j=0}^{n}\chi^{n-j}E\psi_{j}.

Since
\sum_{n}E\psi_{n}<\infty
,
3.2
(part iv.c) ensures
G_{n}\to G^{\star}
strongly in
L^{2}
and a.s., with:

E\|G_{n}-G^{\star}\|_{X}^{2}\leq 4\chi^{n}Ed_{Z,\phi}^{2}(G_{0})+4c\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}\chi^{n-j-1}E\psi_{j}+2c\sum_{j\geq n}E\psi_{j},

yielding a geometric rate
ED_{\phi}(G^{\star},G_{n})=O(\chi^{n})
for all variants, with over-relaxed variants improving the rate via
\lambda_{n}(2-\lambda_{n})
.

∎

C.4.8
Proof of Theorem
5.5
(Mirror-Prox for KL-Regularized RLHF/Policy Learning)

Proof.

Assume
Z\subset\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
,
G_{n}\in\operatorname{int}\mathrm{dom}\phi
a.s., and use Assumption
A.1
(SA1–SA5):
X
is the probability simplex
\{\pi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\mid\pi_{i}\geq 0,\sum_{i}\pi_{i}=1\}
,
\phi(\pi)=\sum_{i}\pi_{i}\log\pi_{i}
is Legendre,
Z
is nonempty and closed,
g_{n}(\cdot)\in L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;X^{*})
is a stochastic subgradient of
f
with
E[g_{n}(y)\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]=\nabla f(y)
a.s.,
\eta_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,+\infty))
, and for over-relaxed variants,
\lambda_{n}\in L^{\infty}(\Omega,\mathcal{F},P;(0,2))
is independent of
\sigma(\{g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\}\cup\Phi_{n})
, where
\Phi_{n}=\{G_{0},\ldots,G_{n}\}
and
\mathcal{X}_{n}=\sigma(\Phi_{n})
. The updates are:

•

Standard Mirror-Prox:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(G_{n})\right),\quad G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\right),

•

Over-Relaxed Type A:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(G_{n})\right),\quad G_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\right),

•

Over-Relaxed Type B:

\tilde{G}_{n+1}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(G_{n})\right),\quad Y_{n}=(\nabla\phi)^{-1}\left(\nabla\phi(G_{n})-\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\right),\quad G_{n+1}=(1-\lambda_{n})G_{n}+\lambda_{n}Y_{n}.

By
B.5
, all updates are special cases of
algorithm

1
with
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(standard or Type B) or
U_{n}u_{n}^{*}=\lambda_{n}\eta_{n}g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
(Type A), and
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(standard) or
Y_{n}(\cdot,z)=\lambda_{n}\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)
(over-relaxed), where
\varepsilon_{n}(\cdot,z)=\frac{1}{2}E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]+\|G_{n}-z\|_{X}\sqrt{E[\|e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})\|_{X}^{2}\mid\mathcal{X}_{n}]}
,
e_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})=g_{n}(\tilde{G}_{n+1})-\nabla f(\tilde{G}_{n+1})
.

Convergence under deterministic
z\in Z
.

Assume
E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}\to 0
a.s.,
\sum_{n}\eta_{n}^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for standard or Type B) or
\sum_{n}(\lambda_{n}\eta_{n})^{2}E\|g_{n}(G_{n})\|_{*}^{2}<\infty
a.s. (for Type A), and
\sum_{n}Y_{n}(\cdot,z)<\infty
a.s. for all
z\in Z
.

(a) Strong convergence of
g_{n}(G_{n})
:
